For printing, I'm planning all tubes vertically on the bed in their collapsed positions, with an outward collar at the bottom of each inner tube and an inward lip at the top of each outer tube to act as stops when extended — using a 45° chamfer on the inward lip to avoid overhangs needing support.\n\nNow I'm working through the clearances: the collar needs to slide inside the outer tube's bore with enough gap for fit, but that pushes the inner tube's main wall further away from the outer tube's wall than the collar itself, so I need to reconcile those radii carefully to keep the sliding fit consistent.\n\nWorking through the overlap math, I settle on s=1.5mm with c=0.5mm clearance giving a 1.0mm radial overlap between collar and lip for adequate stop engagement. Now I need to track the radial budget across nested tubes, starting from a max OD of 25mm (R1_out = 12.5mm) and stepping each subsequent tube's outer radius down by the clearance and stop depth at each nesting level.\n\nTesting wall thickness w=1.6mm gives too thin an innermost tube at R4=1.7mm, so I reduce to w=1.2mm with s=1.2mm and c=0.5mm for a 2.9mm step per level, yielding R1=12.5, R2=9.6, R3=6.7, R4=3.8mm with a 2.6mm inner radius — workable, though the resulting overlap of 0.7mm leaves the collar wall feeling a bit thin.\n\nReconsidering the collar geometry: the lip's inner radius sits at r_o+c and the collar's outer radius at r_o+s, so the overlap is indeed s−c. I also realize the lip-to-inner-tube interface is itself a sliding surface, so it should follow the same 0.4-0.6mm clearance rule — maybe setting c=0.45 instead of 0.5 for consistency.\n\nTesting concrete values w=1.2, s=1.4, c=0.5 gives radii stepping down from 12.5 to 2.0mm with a 0.9mm overlap, which seems workable and keeps the total OD under 25mm. The gap between the inner tube wall and outer tube bore (sized by s) is intentionally larger since it's non-contact, while the collar and lip surfaces keep the tighter clearance c for sliding fit.\n\nI'm now thinking through the stop geometry itself — if both the collar top and lip underside are chamfered at 45°, the matching cones create axial contact that generates a radial wedge force pushing the outer tube to expand, which isn't ideal. Making the collar top flat instead of a cone avoids this wedging effect while still letting the lip underside taper for printability, though I need to check where exactly contact happens on that cone's radius.\n\nAlso thinking about grip access in the collapsed state — if all tubes are flush at the same length with bottoms on the bed, the innermost tube becomes hard to grab since its top sits level with the others. A knob on the top of the innermost tube would need to be wider than that tube, which would overhang above the next tube's lip, so I need another way to make it graspable.\n\nStaggering the tube lengths slightly (110, 113, 116, 119) lets each inner tube protrude a few millimeters above the one outside it, giving a grip point while keeping the collapsed total under 120mm, with all bottoms flush at z=0. I also need to consider whether the bottom needs a stop too, since an inner tube sliding down could potentially slip out the open bottom of its outer tube — the anti-separation requirement should probably apply at both ends, not just at full extension.\n\nActually a 45° outward-expanding cone at the inner tube's top could work as a self-supporting overhang stop, sitting with a gap above the outer tube's lip since the inner tube protrudes past it. Each tube then needs both an inward lip near its top (to stop the next inner tube) and an outward conical flange (to stop itself against the tube below it), which seems consistent across the nested sequence.\n\nThe gap stays safely above the lower tube since the cone height always sits at least gz higher, so there's no fusing risk with just 3 layers of clearance. When tube i+1 descends though, its cone surface will eventually contact tube i's inner top corner at radius r_o+c, allowing roughly gz+c = 1.1mm of travel before that contact occurs.\n\nI'm also weighing whether the downward stop is really necessary -- it's there to prevent the tube from fully sliding out and separating, which seems worth keeping, plus it doubles as a grip ridge. The key constraint is keeping flange outer radius within the 12.5mm OD limit: tube 1 doesn't need a flange since it's outermost, tube 2's flange rests on tube 1's top lip, and tube 3's flange needs to sit on tube 2's own flange structure, which has both outward and inward lips to account for.\n\nI'm working out the top face geometry of each tube: tube i+1's top face is an annulus spanning from its inward lip radius to the flange's outer radius, with the next tube's flange cone resting above that. I'm also recalculating the collapsed-state heights, factoring in the cone transition height plus a vertical band for each flange.\n\nI'm checking the overlap at full extension too, where the bottom collar of tube i+1 should seat against the underside of tube i's lip, with the overlap roughly equal to the collar height plus the lip height.\n\nWith only a short engagement length and the 0.5 mm clearance between collar and bore, the tilt angle could end up noticeably large — maybe around 6° — so I'm considering lengthening the collar, say to 12 mm, to reduce the wobble.\n\nNow I'm laying out the tube lengths with roughly 3 mm step differences (110, 113, 116, 119) and working through how far each tube can extend before the collar's chamfered top contacts the lip's underside, tracking the collar outer radius against the cone geometry.\n\nI'm matching the chamfer and lip cone radii, finding that the cone height on the lip underside comes out equal to the gap s, since Ri_in minus r_o minus c reduces to s.\n\nNow I'm noting both conical surfaces follow a constant z+r relationship, since they're both at 45°. Contact between the collar chamfer and lip chamfer happens when the two z+r constants line up, giving a displacement δ equal to z_l0 + Ri_in minus hc minus r_o — and I need to check how radial offset of the inner tube factors into that.\n\nIf the inner tube shifts off-center by the clearance c, the stop still functions on at least one side since the opposite side's overlap would merely increase while the engaged side's overlap decreases but doesn't vanish entirely, so the lip can't fully escape without deforming. I'm also considering whether flat horizontal stop faces would offer better retention than the 45° cones, but that would require the lip to bridge or cantilever over the small annular gap, which seems geometrically awkward given the tight clearance.\n\nShort overhangs around 1-2mm print fine in PLA but aren't great quality, so I'll keep the 45° cone on the lip's underside and make the collar top flat for easy printing, letting the collar's outer edge contact the cone surface. Actually, matching cones on both sides gives better surface contact, more even force distribution, and a self-centering wedge effect, so I'll go with that approach instead of a flat-to-cone contact.\n\nNow I'm checking the slicer's requirement that adjacent moving surfaces keep a clear nonzero gap — in the collapsed printed state, the collar cone and lip cone sit far apart since the collar is at the bottom and the lip at the top, so I need to verify the minimal Euclidean distance between the flange cone and the tube's top inner corner satisfies the clearance requirement, not just the vertical or horizontal gap components.\n\nComputing the perpendicular distance from the corner point to the 45° cone surface gives about 0.78 mm, which clears the minimum. All parts bottom out at z=0 on the print bed, so nothing floats during printing. The upper limit δ should correspond exactly to where the 45° cone surfaces meet in the nominal coaxial position, and I plan to verify this numerically by checking intersection volumes just below and above δ.\n\nFor verification, I'll export each part as its own STL, then combine them into model.stl by letting OpenSCAD union disjoint shells (since parts never touch, this just concatenates them). I also want to confirm each part is watertight, check volumes, and verify pairwise clearances by computing minimum distances between part surfaces—either through mesh proximity queries or by exploiting the rotational symmetry to do an analytical 2D profile check instead.\n\nI'm thinking the cleanest approach is to slice the exported STL meshes with a plane through the axis using trimesh.section, then use shapely to measure actual distances between the resulting 2D polygons—this verifies the real exported geometry rather than just my design intent. {"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"id":"toolu_bdrk_011QfMGiBCPsF2PNkkFJZin6","input":{"file_path":"/app/output/model.scad","content":"// =====================================================================\n// 4-stage print-in-place telescoping rod (FDM, PLA, 0.4 nozzle, 0.2 layer)\n// Units: mm. Axis = +Z. Printed upright, collapsed, every tube on the bed.\n//\n// Tube 1 = outermost ... tube 4 = innermost.\n// Each inner tube k (k>=2) carries\n//   * a bottom COLLAR that slides in the bore of tube k-1 and hits the\n//     top LIP of tube k-1 at full extension  (pull-out stop),\n//   * a top FLANGE that rests above the top face of tube k-1\n//     (push-through stop, also a finger grip).\n// Each outer tube k (k<=3) carries a top inward LIP.\n// All overhangs (lip underside, flange underside, bottom chamfers) are\n// 45 deg cones, so no support material is needed anywhere.\n//\n// part = 0 -> whole assembly, part = 1..4 -> single tube\n// =====================================================================\n\npart = 0;\n\n/* ---------------- parameters ---------------- */\nN        = 4;          // number of tubes\nOD_max   = 25;         // outer diameter of tube 1\nwall     = 1.2;        // tube wall thickness (3 x 0.4 lines)\nclr      = 0.5;        // radial sliding clearance, per side (0.4-0.6 spec)\nstop_dep = 1.4;        // radial depth of collar / lip (engagement = stop_dep - clr)\nL1       = 107;        // length of tube 1\nstep_L   = 4;          // each inner tube is this much longer (protrudes above)\ncollar_h = 18;         // collar height incl. 45 deg top cone\nlip_h    = 4;          // lip height incl. 45 deg bottom cone\nflange_r = 2.0;        // radial overhang of top flange over tube below\nflange_gz= 0.6;        // axial gap flange underside <-> top of outer tube (3 layers)\nef       = 0.4;        // 45 deg bottom chamfers (elephant-foot relief)\neps      = 0.01;\n$fn      = 160;\n\n/* ---------------- derived ---------------- */\npitch = wall + clr + stop_dep;                 // radius step between tubes\nfunction r_out(k) = OD_max/2 - (k-1)*pitch;    // tube body outer radius\nfunction r_in(k)  = r_out(k) - wall;           // bore radius\nfunction len(k)   = L1 + (k-1)*step_L;\nfunction r_col(k) = r_in(k-1) - clr;           // collar outer radius (k>=2)\nfunction r_lip(k) = r_out(k+1) + clr;          // lip inner radius (k<=N-1)\nfunction r_fl(k)  = r_out(k) + flange_r;       // flange outer radius (k>=2)\n// travel of tube k+1 inside tube k until collar cone meets lip cone\nfunction travel(k) = len(k) - lip_h - collar_h + (r_in(k) - r_out(k+1));\n// drop of tube k+1 until its flange cone touches tube k top inner edge\nfunction drop(k)   = flange_gz + clr;\n\ntotal_ext = len(N) + travel(1) + travel(2) + travel(3);\necho(str(\"collapsed length = \", len(N), \" mm, max OD = \", 2*r_out(1),\n         \" mm (flanges r=\", r_fl(2), \")\"));\necho(str(\"travel 1-2/2-3/3-4 = \", travel(1), \" / \", travel(2), \" / \", travel(3),\n         \" mm, extended length = \", total_ext, \" mm\"));\necho(str(\"radial stop engagement = \", stop_dep - clr, \" mm, innermost ID = \",\n         2*r_in(N), \" mm\"));\n\nassert(2*r_out(1) <= 25, \"OD too large\");\nassert(r_fl(2) <= OD_max/2, \"flange exceeds max OD\");\nassert(len(N) <= 120, \"collapsed length too long\");\nassert(total_ext >= 300, \"extended length too short\");\nassert(clr >= 0.4 && clr <= 0.6, \"clearance out of spec\");\nassert(r_in(N) > 0.5, \"innermost tube has no bore\");\n\n/* ---------------- 2D half-profile (r,z) of tube k ---------------- */\nmodule profile(k) {\n    Ro = r_out(k); Ri = r_in(k); L = len(k);\n    difference() {\n        union() {\n            // body wall\n            translate([Ri, 0]) square([wall, L]);\n            // bottom collar (k>=2): 45 deg bottom chamfer, 45 deg top cone\n            if (k >= 2) {\n                rc = r_col(k);\n                polygon([[Ro - eps, 0], [rc - ef, 0], [rc, ef],\n                         [rc, collar_h - (rc - Ro)], [Ro - eps, collar_h]]);\n            }\n            // top inward lip (k<=N-1): 45 deg cone underneath\n            if (k <= N - 1) {\n                rl = r_lip(k);\n                polygon([[Ri + eps, L - lip_h], [Ri + eps, L], [rl, L],\n                         [rl, L - lip_h + (Ri - rl)]]);\n            }\n            // top outward flange (k>=2): 45 deg cone underneath\n            if (k >= 2) {\n                z0 = len(k-1) + flange_gz;\n                rf = r_fl(k);\n                polygon([[Ro - eps, z0], [rf, z0 + (rf - Ro)], [rf, L],\n                         [Ro - eps, L]]);\n            }\n        }\n        // 45 deg chamfer at the bottom edge of the bore\n        polygon([[Ri - 1, -1], [Ri + ef + 1, -1], [Ri, ef]]);\n        // 45 deg chamfer at the bottom outer edge of tube 1\n        if (k == 1) polygon([[Ro + 1, -1], [Ro - ef - 1, -1], [Ro, ef]]);\n    }\n}\n\nmodule tube(k) rotate_extrude(convexity = 6) profile(k);\n\nif (part == 0) for (k = [1:N]) tube(k);\nelse tube(part);\n"},"name":"Write","type":"tool_use"}],"id":"msg_1ec37b879cd0240b328e1103","model":"claude-opus-5-5","provider_usage":{"provider":"kiro"},"role":"assistant","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"type":"message","usage":{"input_tokens":30773,"output_tokens":0},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ebb1265-7c05-4b56-b2d4-cd69361c26e8","uuid":"fddd5255-f320-4dcb-aba5-c0014910bf45","timestamp":"2026-10-18T09:27:35.013Z"}

`model.scad` | 参数化源码（`part=0` 为整体，`part=1..4` 为单根管） |\n| `model.stl` | 收缩状态（即打印状态）整体模型，4 个互不接触的实体 |\n| `parts/parts_1..4.stl` | 4 根管，1 = 最外管，4 = 最内管，与 `model.stl` 同一坐标系 |\n| `joints.json` | 3 个移动副：轴向、行程、限位关系 |\n| `section.png` | 轴向剖面图：收缩状态、伸展状态、顶部细节 |\n| `checks/` | 检查脚本与日志（`geom_check.*`、`gcode_check.*`） |\n\n## 结构\n- 所有管子底面都在 z=0。内层管依次加长 4 mm（107/111/115/119 mm），伸出的部分可以用手指捏住拉出。\n- 外管 Ø25 mm，壁厚 1.2 mm。各管外径依次为 25 / 18.8 / 12.6 / 6.4 mm，最内管内径 4 mm。\n- **拉出限位**：内管底部有一圈高 18 mm 的套环，在外管内孔中滑动；外管顶部有一圈向内的限位唇。两者都做成 45° 锥面，伸到底时锥面贴合，径向搭接 0.9 mm。\n- **压入限位**：内管顶部有一圈 45° 外凸缘，打印时它与外管顶面之间留有 0.6 mm 轴向间隙（3 层）。往下压 1.1 mm 后凸缘碰到外管顶面，管子不会从底部滑出。\n- **间隙**：两处滑动配合面（套环外壁对外管内孔、内管管身对限位唇）单侧径向间隙均为 **0.5 mm**。打印状态下各限位面之间的最小间隙也是 0.5 mm（不为零）。\n- 底部边缘做了 0.4 mm×45° 倒角，抵消首层\"象脚\"，首层上的实际间隙约 1.1 mm。\n\n## 打印方向\n**直立打印**：轴线竖直，z=0 端贴热床，按收缩状态一次打印完成。不加支撑，不需要组装。\n\n4 根管各自从热床长起。所有悬垂面都是 45° 锥面（下唇锥面、凸缘下表面、底部倒角），没有水平悬空面。管与管之间的间隙全程是竖直的空槽，不需要支撑。打印完成后，依次捏住各管顶部凸缘拉出即可（如有轻微粘连，先转动一下）。\n\n## 已完成的检查（日志见 `checks/`）\n**几何检查**（`geom_check.py`，基于导出的 STL，使用 trimesh + manifold3d + shapely）\n- 每个零件都是水密的单一实体；`model.stl` 由 4 个实体组成，体积等于各零件之和（28695.5 mm³）。外形尺寸 25×25×119 mm。\n- 打印状态下任意两根管的布尔交集体积都为 0。\n- 轴向剖面上相邻两管最小间隙为 0.500 mm；套环与内孔、限位唇与管身的径向间隙实测均为 0.500 mm。\n- 运动扫描：内管沿 Z 每 0.5 mm 检测一次干涉，结果如下。\n\n  | 移动副 | 行程范围（mm） |\n  |---|---|\n  | 1-2 | −1.11 … +86.91 |\n  | 2-3 | −1.11 … +90.91 |\n  | 3-4 | −1.11 … +94.91 |\n\n  行程内无干涉，与设计值一致。超出两端限位后都会产生干涉（即被挡住）；内管横向偏移 ±0.49 mm 的最坏情况下也同样被挡住，管子不会脱出。\n- 完全伸展：4 根管两两无干涉，总长 **391.6 mm**（≥300），每一级伸到底时套接长度为 20.1 mm。\n- 悬垂分析：朝下的面最平也是 45°，水平悬空面数量为 0。\n\n**切片检查**（PrusaSlicer 2.7.2，`/opt/slicer` 中的 P1S PLA 0.2 mm 配置）\n- `nosupport` 配置：595 层，耗时约 4 h 16 min，耗材 27.3 cm³。\n- `autosupport` 配置（自动支撑打开，阈值为自动）：**没有生成任何支撑**（G-code 中没有 `Support material`）。\n- 逐层解析 G-code（`gcode_check.py`）：按线宽计算挤出线条外缘的径向位置，每层相邻管之间都保留独立间隙。最小间隙 0.453 mm（z=9.0），首层 1.07 mm。两处桥接填充（z=110.2 和 114.2）位于管 2、管 3 顶部实心区域内部，没有跨越管与管之间的间隙。\n\n## 注意事项\n- 0.5 mm 间隙下，伸展到底时每一级约有 2–3° 的晃动，这是间隙要求带来的固有结果。\n- 若打印机挤出偏多，可以把 `clr` 调到 0.6；若希望更紧，可调到 0.4，然后重新导出。\n"},"name":"Write","type":"tool_use"}],"id":"msg_fe8f084d21d4eb5705e5a145","model":"claude-opus-5-5","provider_usage":{"provider":"kiro"},"role":"assistant","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"type":"message","usage":{"input_tokens":70020,"output_tokens":0},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ebb1265-7c05-4b56-b2d4-cd69361c26e8","uuid":"2580dc6c-b999-4511-abc1-f04ca138d710","timestamp":"2026-10-18T09:32:50.122Z"}
